3)

Any component with domain
[0,1]
which is homotopic relative endpoints to a subset of
\partial{\bf S}\backslash M
.

The relations in 1) and 2) are understood to describe local relations inside a small disc (indicated by the dotted circle) in
{\bf S}
, applicable to any subset of components of a link. The depicted curves are identical outside the small disc.

Remark 7.11
.

The
q=1
Kauffman Skein relation recover the cluster exchange relations given by the Ptolemy relation, see
Definition

7.7
.

We remark that
Definition

7.10
is equivalent to the definition of the
q
-Skein algebra given by Muller in
[
Mul16
]
, with
q
set to
1
, as can be seen by using Remarks 3.4 and 3.6 in
[
Mul16
]
.

Definition 7.12
.

We define the localized
q=1
Skein algebra
\operatorname{Sk}^{1,\operatorname{loc}}({\bf S})
as the localization of
\operatorname{Sk}^{1}({\bf S})
at the set of boundary arcs.

Theorem 7.13

(
[
Mul16
]
)
.

There exist injective morphisms of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebras

\operatorname{CA}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{loc}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Sk}^{1,\operatorname{loc}}({\bf S})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{UCA}_{\bf S}\,.

(16)

If
{\bf S}
additionally has at least two marked points, then the maps in (
16
) are equivalences of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebras.

7.3
Classification of cluster tilting objects

We choose a trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
of
{\bf S}
. Consider the perverse schober
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\operatorname{clst}}
from
Definition

4.2
with its
k[t_{2}^{\pm}]
-linear smooth and proper
\infty
-category of global sections given by the generalized cluster category
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
. We let
G
be the right adjoint of the adjunction

F\coloneqq\partial\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{clst}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\colon\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{T}}_{1}^{\partial}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\operatorname{clst}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}(e)\longleftrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}G\coloneqq\prod_{e\in{\mathcal{T}}_{1}^{\partial}}\operatorname{ev}_{e}

from
Definition

3.12
. Since
\operatorname{char}(k)\neq 2
, the functor
G
admits a
2
-Calabi–Yau structure, see
Theorem

4.17
and the adjunction
F\dashv G
is spherical by
Corollary

4.18
. By
Section

5.3
, we obtain a Frobenius,
2
-Calabi–Yau extrianguled category
(\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}},\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}},\mathfrak{s})
arising from of a Frobenius exact
\infty
-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
.

Theorem 7.14
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field with
\operatorname{char}(k)\neq 2
.

i)

Let
\gamma
be a matching curve in
{\bf S}
. Then
M_{\gamma}
is rigid in
(\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}},\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}},\mathfrak{s})
if and only if
\gamma
is an arc.

ii)

Consider a collection
I
of distinct arcs in
{\bf S}
. Then
\bigoplus_{\gamma\in I}M_{\gamma}
is a cluster tilting object in
(\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}},\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}},\mathfrak{s})
if and only if
I
is an ideal triangulation of
{\bf S}
.

We prove
Theorem

7.14
further below.

Corollary 7.15
.

There are canonical bijections between the sets of the following objects.

•

Clusters of the cluster algebra with coefficients
\operatorname{CA}_{\bf S}
of
{\bf S}
.

•

Ideal triangulations of
{\bf S}
.

•

Cluster tilting objects in
(\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}},\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}},\mathfrak{s})
up to equivalence.

Proof.

Combining the geometrization
Theorem

6.12
and
Theorem

7.14
, we obtain that there is a bijection between the sets of equivalence classes of cluster tilting objects and ideal triangulations of
{\bf S}
. The bijection between clusters and ideal triangulation is
Theorem

7.8
.
∎

Lemma 7.16
.

(1)

Let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
be two distinct matching curves in
{\bf S}
. Then

\operatorname{Mor}^{\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})\subset\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})

consists of those direct summands identified in
Theorem

6.7
which correspond to crossings of
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
.

(2)

Let
\gamma
be an open matching curve in
{\bf S}
. Then

\operatorname{Mor}^{\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})\subset\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})

consists of those direct summands identified in
Theorem

6.8
which corresponding to self-crossings.

(3)

Let
\gamma
be a closed matching curve in
{\bf S}
. Then

\operatorname{Mor}^{\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})=\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})\,.

Proof.

Inspecting the construction of the direct summands of
\operatorname{Mor}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
corresponding to the different types of intersections in
[
Chr21
]
, one finds the following.

•

Boundary intersections give rise to morphisms which evaluate non-trivially at an external edge of
\mathcal{T}
, i.e. do not get mapped by
G
to zero.

•

Crossings give rise to morphisms which restrict to zero on all external edges of
\mathcal{T}
and thus define direct summands lying in
\operatorname{Mor}^{\operatorname{CY}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
.

•

If
\gamma
is open, then the direct summand
k[t_{1}^{\pm}]\subset\operatorname{Mor}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})
does not evaluate to zero under
G
.

•

If
\gamma
is closed, then
G(M_{\gamma})\simeq 0
and hence
\operatorname{Mor}^{\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})=\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})
.

This shows the Lemma.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

7.14
.

We begin with part i). If
\gamma
is a closed matching curve, then
M_{\gamma}
is not rigid because there is always a direct summand
k[t_{1}^{\pm}]\subset\operatorname{Mor}^{\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})=\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})
. We thus assume that
\gamma
is open.
Theorem

6.7
and
Lemma

7.16
imply that
M_{\gamma}
is rigid if and only if
\gamma
has no self crossings, meaning that
\gamma
is an arc, showing part i).

Using
Theorem

6.7
and
Lemma

7.16
, we find that given two arcs
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
, we have an isomorphism
\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})\simeq 0
if and only if
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
are compatible in the sense of
Definition

6.5
. By part i) and the maximality of an ideal triangulation, we find that any basic, maximal rigid object is of the form
\bigoplus_{\gamma\in I}M_{\gamma}
for an ideal triangulation
I
and conversely any ideal triangulation
I
gives rise to such a basic, maximal rigid object. Since any cluster tilting object is basic and maximal rigid, it remains to verify that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(\bigoplus_{\gamma\in I}M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})\not\simeq 0

(17)

for
I
an ideal triangulation and
\gamma^{\prime}\notin I
a matching curve.

Let thus
I
be an ideal triangulation. Then
I
decomposes
{\bf S}
into triangles with edges the arcs in
I
. If a matching curve
\gamma^{\prime}
crosses an edge of one of these triangles, we find a direct summand
k[t_{1}^{\pm}]\subset\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}^{\operatorname{CY}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
, showing (
17
). If
\gamma^{\prime}
does not cross any arcs in
I
, then
\gamma^{\prime}
is contained in an ideal triangle and hence already in
I
. We thus obtain that (
17
) holds for all
\gamma^{\prime}\notin I
, showing that
\bigoplus_{\gamma\in I}M_{\gamma}
is cluster tilting.
∎

Recall that given a trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
of
{\bf S}
, we denote the associated relative Ginzburg algebra by
\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proposition 7.17
.

Consider an ideal triangulation
I
of
{\bf S}
with dual trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
and let
X=\bigoplus_{\gamma\in I}M_{\gamma}
be the associated cluster tilting object.

(1)

The Jacobian algebra
\mathscr{J}_{\mathcal{T}}\coloneqq\operatorname{H}_{0}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})
is a gentle algebra.

(2)

There exists an isomorphism of dg-algebras (with vanishing differentials)

\operatorname{H}_{*}\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(X,X)\simeq\operatorname{H}_{0}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})\otimes_{k}k[t_{1}^{\pm}]\,.

In particular, the discrete endomorphism algebra
\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(X,X)
of
X
is a gentle algebra.

Proof.

Part (1) is observed in
[
Chr21
, Section 7]
. Part (2) follows from
Theorems

6.7
and
6.8
as in the proof of
[
Chr21
, Prop. 7.3]
.
∎

Remark 7.18
.

The marked surface associated to the Jacobian gentle algebra
\operatorname{H}_{0}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})
(in the sense of
[
OPS18
, Def 1.10]
) is given by the complement in
{\bf S}
of the set of vertices of the trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
. This gentle algebra is finite dimensional but not smooth.

Example 7.19
.

Consider the
4
-gon
{\bf S}
, depicted as follows, with an ideal triangulation (in blue).

The generalized cluster category
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
is equivalent to the
1
-periodic derived
\infty
-category of the
A_{3}
-quiver, which can be defined as
\mathcal{D}(kA_{3}\otimes_{k}k[t_{1}^{\pm}])
. The arising Jacobian gentle algebra is given by quotient of the path algebra of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}

by the ideal
(ba,cb,ac,b^{\prime}a^{\prime},c^{\prime}b^{\prime},a^{\prime}c^{\prime})
.

Example 7.20
.

Consider the annulus
{\bf S}
with a marked point on each boundary component and an ideal triangulation depicted as follows.

The Jacobian gentle algebra is given by the quotient of the path algebra of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}}

by the ideal
(ba,cb,ac,b^{\prime}a^{\prime},c^{\prime}b^{\prime},a^{\prime}c^{\prime})
. This is a relative version of the Kronecker quiver. While the mapping class group of the
4
-gon in
Example

7.19
is trivial, the mapping class group of the annulus
{\bf S}
is given by
\mathbb{Z}
. The generator
1\in\mathbb{Z}
corresponds to a diffeomorphism rotating one boundary circle by one full rotation and fixing the other boundary circle. Under the action of an element
\alpha
of the mapping class group, an object
M_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
is mapped to
M_{\alpha\circ\gamma}
.

7.4
A cluster character on
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}

We begin with the notion of a cluster character on an extriangulated category, generalizing the notion of a cluster character of
[
Pal08
]
.

Definition 7.21
.

Let
(C,\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be a
k
-linear extriangulated category. We denote by
\operatorname{obj}(C)
the set of equivalence classes of objects in
C
. A cluster character
\chi
on
C
with values in a commutative ring
R
is a map

\chi\colon\operatorname{obj}(C)\rightarrow R

such that for all
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
the following holds.

•

\chi(X)=\chi(Y)
if
X\simeq Y
.

•

\chi(X\oplus Y)=\chi(X)\chi(Y)\,
.

•

\chi(X)\chi(Y)=\chi(B)+\chi(B^{\prime})
if
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\mathbb{E}(X,Y)=\operatorname{dim}_{k}\mathbb{E}(Y,X)=1
and
B,B^{\prime}\in C
are the middle terms of the corresponding non-split extensions of
X
by
Y
and
Y
by
X
.

The last property is called the cluster multiplication formula.

Given a matching curve
\gamma
in
{\bf S}
, we consider the underlying curve as a link in
{\bf S}
with a single component, denoted
l(\gamma)
.

Theorem 7.22
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field with
\operatorname{char}(k)\neq 2
. Consider the map

\chi\colon\operatorname{obj}(\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Sk}^{1}_{\bf S}

determined by

•

\chi(A)=\chi(B)
if
A\simeq B

•

\chi(A\oplus B)=\chi(A)\chi(B)

•

\chi(M_{\gamma})=l(\gamma)
for any matching curve
\gamma
in
{\bf S}
.

Then
\chi
is a cluster character on
(\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}},\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}},\mathfrak{s})
.

Remark 7.23
.

Composing
\chi
with
\operatorname{Sk}^{1}_{\bf S}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Sk}^{1,\operatorname{loc}}_{\bf S}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{UCA}_{\bf S}
defines a cluster character to the upper cluster algebra of
{\bf S}
. If
\mathbb{S}
has at least two marked points, the upper cluster algebra is equivalent to the cluster algebra with coefficients
\operatorname{CA}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{loc}}
localized at the boundary arcs, see
Theorem

7.13
.

Proof of
Theorem

7.22
.

The geometrization
Theorem

6.12
shows that
\chi
is well-defined. It is also clear that
\chi
satisfies all parts of
Definition

7.21
, except for the cluster multiplication formula.
We thus consider two matching curves
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
in
{\bf S}
, which satisfy
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})=1
and let
B,B^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
be the corresponding extensions. Suppose that
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
are distinct. By
Theorem

6.7
,
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})=1
implies that
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
have a single crossing. The cluster multiplication formula
\chi(M_{\gamma})\chi(M_{\gamma^{\prime}})=\chi(B)+\chi(B^{\prime})
thus follows from
Proposition

6.9
, see also
Remark

6.10
.

If
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}
, we find that
\gamma
is closed, and that
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
have the same monodromy datum. Denote by
a
and
a^{\prime}
the ranks of
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
. We show that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
is always an even number and hence not equal to
1
. Self-crossings of
\gamma
give rise to two crossings of
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
and thus contribute the number
2aa^{\prime}
to
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
. The global section
M_{\gamma}
arises as a coequalizer, see (
15
). It follows that
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
is equivalent to the fiber of some morphism

k[t_{1}^{\pm}]^{\oplus aa^{\prime}}\longrightarrow k[t_{1}^{\pm}]^{\oplus aa^{\prime}+2aa^{\prime}i^{\operatorname{cr}}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\,.

(18)

Inspecting the construction of the morphism (
18
), one finds that it lies in the image of the functor
\zeta^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}(k[t_{1}^{\pm}])\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(k[t_{2}^{\pm}])
. Using that every morphism in
\mathcal{D}(k[t_{1}^{\pm}])
splits into a direct sum of an equivalence and a zero morphism, it follows that
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}^{\operatorname{CY}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})=\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})
is free of even rank in
\mathcal{D}(k[t_{1}^{\pm}])
, which implies that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1,\operatorname{CY}}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma})
is indeed an even number. This concludes the case distinction and the proof.
∎

Remark 7.24
.

The proof of
Theorem

7.22
shows that there is some flexibility in the formula for the cluster character. In fact, it appears that one can assign arbitrary values to the closed matching curves of rank
a\geq 2
.

8
Further directions

In
Section

8.1
, we recall the construction of the Higgs category
[
Wu23
]
and show that the
1
-periodic topological Fukaya category
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
is equivalent as an exact
\infty
-category to the Higgs category. From this, it follows that the (homotopy category of the) stable category of
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
recovers the standard triangulated
2
-Calabi–Yau cluster category of the marked surface
{\bf S}
. In
Section

8.2
, we observe that the mapping class group action on
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
induces a mapping class group action on this triangulated
2
-Calabi–Yau cluster category. Finally, in
Section

8.3
, we describe the generalized
n
-cluster categories arising from relative higher Ginzburg algebras associated with
n
-angulated marked surfaces.

8.1
Relation with the Higgs category

In
[
Wu23
]
, Yilin Wu describes a generalization of Amiot’s construction of the generalized cluster category, that is different to the approach of this paper and considers a more general setup. Given the input of suitable relative left
3
-Calabi–Yau dg-algebra, the result is a
2
-Calabi–Yau Frobenius extriangulated category equipped with cluster tilting objects. In the following, we recall this construction in the special case where the relative
3
-Calabi–Yau dg-algebra is the relative Ginzburg algebras associated with an ice quiver with potential and show the compatibility with the approach of this paper if the ice quiver with potential comes from a triangulated marked surface.

Let
(Q,F)
be a finite ice quiver, meaning that
Q
is a finite quiver and
F\subset Q
a (not necessarily full) sub-quiver. Let
W
be a potential for
Q
. Associated with this is the relative Ginzburg algebra
\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)
and the Ginzburg dg-functor
[
Wu23
]

{\bf Gi}\colon\Pi_{2}(kF)\to\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)\,,

where
\Pi_{2}(kF)
denotes the
2
-Calabi–Yau completion
[
Kel11
]
of the path algebra of
F
. The Ginzburg functor
{\bf Gi}
is equivalent to the deformed relative Calabi–Yau completion of
kF\to kQ
and thus admits a left
3
-Calabi–Yau structure
[
Yeu16
,
BCS20
]
. We will assume that the relative Jacobian algebra
H_{0}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
is finite dimensional and refer to
[
KW23
]
for a discussion of Higgs categories in the Jacobi-infinite case.

Pulling back along
{\bf Gi}
yields the functor

{\bf Gi}^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))\to\mathcal{D}(\Pi_{2}(kF))\,.

Its left adjoint is denoted
{\bf Gi}_{!}
and given by tensoring with the bimodule
\bigoplus_{x\in F_{0}}P_{x}
given by the sum of the indecomposable direct summands of
\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)
associated with the frozen vertices.

We let
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}_{F}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))\subset\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
be the kernel, i.e. the fiber, of the functor
{\bf Gi}^{*}
. The relative cluster category is defined as the Verdier quotient

\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)\coloneqq\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}_{F}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))\,.

To define the Higgs category, we must first recall the definition of the relative fundamental domain
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}\subset\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
. Consider the set
\mathcal{P}
of objects in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
given by the indecomposable direct summands of
\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)
associated with the frozen vertices of
Q
. Note that
\operatorname{Add}(\mathcal{P})
is the essential image
{\bf Gi}_{!}(\operatorname{Add}(kF))
of
\operatorname{Add}(kF)
under the functor
{\bf Gi}_{!}
.

Definition 8.1
.

The relative fundamental domain
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}\subset\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
is the full subcategory spanned by objects
X
satisfying that

1)

X
fits into a fiber and cofiber sequence
M_{1}\to M_{0}\to X
with
M_{0},M_{1}
lying in the additive closure
\operatorname{Add}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
and

2)

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(P,X)\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(X,P)\simeq 0
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
i>0
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Suppose that the functor
{\bf Gi}^{*}
admits a colimit preserving right adjoint
{\bf Gi}_{*}
satisfying that
\operatorname{Add}(\mathcal{P})={\bf Gi}_{*}(\operatorname{Add}(\Pi_{2}(kF)))
. Then condition 2) in
Definition

8.1
is equivalent to
X
satisfying
{\bf Gi}^{*}(X)\in\operatorname{Add}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
.

Note that the condition that
{\bf Gi}_{*}
preserves colimits is equivalent to
{\bf Gi}^{*}
preserving compact objects, i.e. perfect modules.

Proof of
Lemma

8.2
.

Let
X\in\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
. Let
P\simeq{\bf Gi}_{!}(Q)\simeq{\bf Gi}_{*}(Q^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Add}(\mathcal{P})
with
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Add}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
. We have

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))}(P,X)\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))}(Q,{\bf Gi}^{*}(X))

and

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))}(X,P)\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))}({\bf Gi}^{*}(X),Q^{\prime})\,.

Since the dg-algebra
\Pi_{2}(kF)
is concentrated in positive degrees (in the homological grading convention), it defines a silting object in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
. It follows that
\operatorname{Gi}^{*}(X)\in\operatorname{Add}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
holds if and only if

\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))}(\operatorname{Gi}^{*}(X),\Pi_{2}(kF))\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))}(\Pi_{2}(kF),\operatorname{Gi}^{*}(X))\simeq 0

for all
i>0
, see
[
MSSS13
, Thm. 5.5]
or also
[
IY18
, Lem. 2.5, Prop. 2.8]
,
[
KN13
, Thm. 10.2.2)]
. By the above, this is satisfied if and only if
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(P,X)\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(X,P)\simeq 0
for all
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
i>0
.
∎

The Higgs category
\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)
is defined as the image of
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}
under the quotient functor
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))\to\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)
. The arising functor
\phi\colon\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}\to\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
is furthermore fully faithful on the level of homotopy categories
[
Wu23
, Prop. 5.20]
. The Higgs category is an extension closed subcategory of
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)
and thus inherits the structure of an exact
\infty
-category. The homotopy category
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
is therefore extriangulated. Furthermore, the image of
\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)
in
\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
is a cluster tilting object.

We next discuss under the assumptions of
Lemma

8.2
the relation of the Higgs category with the generalized cluster category

\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)\coloneqq\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))\,.

We equip
\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
with the exact
\infty
-structure obtained from pulling back as in
Example

5.8
the split exact structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
along the functor induced by
{\bf Gi}^{*}
(note that
{\bf Gi}^{*}
automatically preserves finite modules and we have assumed that it preserves perfect modules):

\widetilde{{\bf Gi}}^{*}\colon\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))\,.

Composing the inclusion
\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)\subset\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)
with the quotient functor
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
to the generalized cluster category yields the functor

\tau\colon\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)\,.

Proposition 8.3
.

Suppose that the functor
{\bf Gi}^{*}
admits a colimit preserving right adjoint
{\bf Gi}_{*}
satisfying that
\operatorname{Add}(\mathcal{P})={\bf Gi}_{*}(\operatorname{Add}(\Pi_{2}(kF)))
. Then the functor
\tau
is an exact functor between exact
\infty
-categories and thus also gives rise to an extriangulated functor between the extriangulated homotopy categories.

Proof of
Proposition

8.3
.

Since zero objects, inflations, deflations and exact sequences are detected on the level of the extriangulated homotopy categories, the functor
\tau
can be lifted to an exact functor between exact
\infty
-categories if and only if
\operatorname{ho}\tau
can be lifted to an extriangulated functor between the extriangulated homotopy categories. Lifting
\operatorname{ho}\tau\colon\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)\to\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
to an extriangulated functor amounts to specifying a natural transformation between functors
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)^{\operatorname{op}}\times\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)\to\operatorname{Vect}_{k}

\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{ho}\!\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)}(\mathchar 45\relax,\mathchar 45\relax)\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{ho}\!\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)}(\tau(\mathchar 45\relax),\tau(\mathchar 45\relax))\,,

such that
\tau
applied to the realization
\mathfrak{s}(\alpha)
of an extension
\alpha
yields the realization
\mathfrak{s}(\tau(\alpha))
.

The fiber and cofiber sequence preserving functor
\tau
induces a natural transformation
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)}(\mathchar 45\relax,\mathchar 45\relax)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)}(\tau(\mathchar 45\relax),\tau(\mathchar 45\relax))
. Since
\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{ho}\!\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)}=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{ho}\!\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)}
and

\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{ho}\!\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)}=\operatorname{ker}(\widetilde{\bf Gi}^{*}|_{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}})\subset\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{ho}\!\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)}

it suffices to show that
\widetilde{{\bf Gi}}^{*}\circ\tau
vanishes on extensions to obtain the desired natural transformation.

Denote by
(\bar{Q},\bar{W})
the (non-ice) quiver with potential obtained from
(Q,F,W)
by removing
F
from
Q
and all cycles from
W
which contain frozen arrows. Let
\mathscr{G}(\bar{Q},\bar{W})
be the corresponding non-relative Ginzburg algebra and
\mathcal{C}(\bar{Q},\bar{W})=\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(\bar{Q},\bar{W}))/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G}(\bar{Q},\bar{W}))
the cluster category. There is a cofiber sequence of compactly generated stable
\infty
-categories, see
[
Wu23
, Prop. 7.8]
,

\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(F))\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))\xlongrightarrow{\pi}\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G}(\bar{Q},\bar{W}))\,.

The functor
\pi
induces the functor
\bar{\pi}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)\to\mathcal{C}(\bar{Q},\bar{W})
.

Let
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
and
Y_{1},Y_{2}\in\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}
denote the images of
X_{1},X_{2}
under the equivalence
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}\simeq\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
. Let further
Y_{1}^{\prime}=\tau_{\leq-1}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Y_{1})
be the relative truncation of
Y_{1}
, see
[
Wu23
, Def. 4.9]
. There is a morphism
Y_{1}^{\prime}\to Y_{1}
, whose cofiber lies in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}_{F}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
, see loc. cit.. The image of the morphism in
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q,F,W)
is thus equivalence. We have the following equivalences,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}(\bar{Q},\bar{W})}(\bar{\pi}(X_{1}),\bar{\pi}(X_{2}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}(\bar{Q},\bar{W}))}(\pi(Y_{1}^{\prime}),\pi(Y_{1}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)}(Y_{1}^{\prime},Y_{2})

where the first equivalence is
[
Wu23
, Prop. 5.36]
, the second equivalence is shown in step 2 in the proof of Proposition 2.12 in
[
Ami09
]
, using that
\pi(\tau^{\operatorname{rel}}_{\leq-1}(Y_{1}))\simeq\tau_{\leq-1}\pi(Y_{1})
, and the last equivalence is shown in
[
Wu23
, Lem. 5.16, Lem. 5.29]
.

Any extension
\alpha\colon X_{1}\to X_{2}[1]
in
\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
thus lifts to an extension
\alpha^{\prime}\colon Y_{1}^{\prime}\to Y_{2}[1]
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W))
. We note that
\widetilde{\bf Gi}^{*}\circ\tau(\alpha)
is equivalent to the image under the quotient functor
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))\to\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
of
{\bf Gi}^{*}(\alpha^{\prime})
. Since
{\bf Gi}^{*}(Y_{1}^{\prime}),{\bf Gi}^{*}(Y_{2})\in\operatorname{Add}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
and
\Pi_{2}(kF)
is rigid, we find that
{\bf Gi}^{*}(\alpha^{\prime})
must vanish. It follows that
\widetilde{\bf Gi}^{*}\circ\tau(\alpha)\simeq 0
, as desired.
∎

The functor
\tau
considered in
Proposition

8.3
is not for every choice of ice quiver with potential an equivalence of extriangulated categories and the generalized cluster category
\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
does not necessarily categorify the corresponding cluster algebra with coefficients. This is because
\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
may vanish for an arbitrary choice of ice quiver with potential. This is the case for instance if
\mathscr{G}(Q,F,W)
is Jacobi-finite and concentrated in degree
0
, see
[
Wu23
]
for examples. For a basic example where
\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
vanishes, see
Example

8.8
.

Finally, we consider the specific choice of ice quiver with potential
(Q,F,W)=(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})
associated with a marked surface
{\bf S}
with an ideal triangulation
I
dual to a trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
. The vertices of
Q_{\mathcal{T}}
are given by the arcs in
I
and the arrows are obtained by inscribing clockwise
3
-cycles into the faces of the ideal triangulation. The frozen part
F_{\mathcal{T}}
consists of the vertices given by boundary arcs. The potential
W_{\mathcal{T}}
is the sum of the
3
-cycles inscribed into the faces. The corresponding non-frozen quiver with potential
(\bar{Q},\bar{W})
was defined in
[
LF09
]
. We note that there is an isomorphisms of dg-algebras
\mathscr{G}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})\simeq\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}}
and thus an equivalence
\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
with the subcategory of compact objects of the
(\operatorname{Ind}
-complete)
1
-periodic topological Fukaya
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
of
{\bf S}
.

Theorem 8.4
.

The functor

\tau\colon\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}

is an equivalence of exact
\infty
-categories. In particular, it induces an equivalence between the extriangulated homotopy categories.

Proof.

The conditions of
Proposition

8.3
are satisfied by
Remark

8.5
below. We first show that
\operatorname{ho}\tau
is an equivalence of extriangulated homotopy categories using
Lemma

8.6
below.

Under the quotient functors
\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})\to\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}}),\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
, the relative Ginzburg algebra is mapped to cluster tilting objects
T\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})
and
T^{\prime}\simeq\bigoplus_{\gamma\in I}M_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
, respectively. The Homs assemble into an apparent commutative diagram, where
\gamma
is induced by the functor
\tau
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}},\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})}(T,T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(T^{\prime},T^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

The morphism
\beta
is an equivalence by the fully faithfulness of the functor
\phi\colon\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}\to\mathcal{H}(Q,F,W)
. Using the geometric description of the endomorphisms of
\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}}
in
[
Chr21
, Thm. 6.4,6.5]
and of
T^{\prime}
in
Theorems

6.7
and
6.8
, it is straightforward to check that
\alpha
is also an equivalence. This implies that the map
\gamma
is also an equivalence.

Next, we show that
\tau
is essentially surjective. Consider an indecomposable object
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
. By
Theorem

6.12
, we have an equivalence
X\simeq M_{\gamma}
for a matching curve
\gamma
in
{\bf S}
. The matching curve
\gamma
lifts uniquely to a pure matching datum
(\gamma,k[t_{1}])
in
{\bf S}\backslash M
in the sense of
[
Chr21
, Def. 5.5]
, see
[
Chr21
, Lem. 5.8]
. The corresponding object
M_{\gamma}^{k[t_{1}]}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})
lies in
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}
and its image in
\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})
is the desired lift of
M_{\gamma}
.

We have shown that
\operatorname{ho}\tau
is an equivalence on extriangulated homotopy categories. The functor
\tau
is thus also an exact functor between exact
\infty
-categories and further gives bijections between the subsets of morphisms given by inflations and deflations. To prove that
\tau
is an equivalence of exact
\infty
-categories, it remains to show that
\tau
is fully faithful, i.e. gives rise to equivalences between the mapping spaces, or equivalently, on all negative extensions groups. Consider two objects
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}
with images
Y,Y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})
. By the equivalence of homotopy categories
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{rel}}\simeq\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
, there exist equivalences
X\simeq M_{\gamma}^{k[t_{1}]}
and
X^{\prime}\simeq M_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{k[t_{1}]}
for two pure matching data
(\gamma,k[t_{1}])
,
(\gamma^{\prime},k[t_{1}])
in
{\bf S}\backslash M
. Note that
\tau(Y)\simeq M_{\gamma}
and
\tau(Y^{\prime})\simeq M_{\gamma^{\prime}}
. The negative extension groups organize into the following commutative diagram,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ext}^{-i}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})}(X,X^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ext}^{-i}_{\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{rel}}(Q_{\mathcal{T}},F_{\mathcal{T}},W_{\mathcal{T}})}(Y,Y^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ext}^{-i}_{\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}}(M_{\gamma},M_{\gamma^{\prime}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the left morphism is an isomorphism by the geometric description of the extension groups in Theorems 6.4 and 6.5 in
[
Chr21
]
, using that
\operatorname{H}_{i}(k[t_{1}])\simeq H_{i}(k[t_{1}^{\pm}])\simeq k
for all
i>0
. The upper morphism is also an isomorphism, as shown in
[
Che23
, Lem. 6.70]
. This shows the desired fully faithfulness.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

The conditions of
Proposition

8.3
are satisfied for the relative Ginzburg algebra
\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}}
, which can be seen as follows. The functor
{\bf Gi}_{!}
is described geometrically in
[
Chr21
, Prop 5.19]
. A minor variation of that argument (where one glues the left adjoints instead of the right adjoints) yields a similar description of the functor
{\bf Gi}_{*}
. In the notation of loc. cit., we find for every edge
e
of the trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
that
M^{k[t_{1}]}_{c_{e}^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{ev}^{!}(k[t_{1}])
, with
\operatorname{ev}^{!}
the right adjoint of
\operatorname{ev}_{e}
(instead of the left adjoint), and
c_{e}^{\prime}
the pure matching curve consisting of segments which each wrap exactly one step clockwise (instead of counterclockwise) around a vertex of
\mathcal{T}
. If
e
is an external edge, then
c_{e}^{\prime}
is a boundary arc, and all boundary arcs arise this way. This relation between the left and right adjoints of
{\bf Gi}^{*}
can also be explained by noting that
{\bf Gi}^{*}
is a spherical functor whose cotwist functor permutes the components of
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\Pi_{2}(kF))
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
\tau\colon(C,\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})\to(C^{\prime},\mathbb{E}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})
be an extriangulated functor between extriangulated categories, such that
C
is Frobenius with finite dimensional Homs and extriangulated
2
-Calabi–Yau. Assume further that

1)

there are cluster tilting objects
T\in C
and
T^{\prime}\in C^{\prime}
,

2)

\tau(T)=T^{\prime}
,

3)

\tau\colon\operatorname{Hom}_{C}(T,T)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{C^{\prime}}(T^{\prime},T^{\prime})
is an isomorphism and

4)

the functor
\tau
is essentially surjective.

Then
\tau
is an extriangulated equivalence.

Remark 8.7
.

A version of
Lemma

8.6
for triangulated categories (without the essential surjectivity assumption) was proven in section 4.5 of
[
KR08
]

Proof of
Lemma

8.6
.

Let
X\in C
and
Y\in\operatorname{Add}(T)
. By
Lemma

5.16
, there exist exact sequences
T_{1}\to T_{0}\to X
and
X\to S_{0}\to S_{1}
in
C
with
T_{0},T_{1},S_{0},S_{1}\in\operatorname{Add}(T)
. We get two morphisms between sequences of vector spaces which are exact by
[
GNP21
, Thm. 3.5]
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(Y,T_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(Y,T_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(Y,X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(Y,T_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(Y,T_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(Y),\tau(T_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(Y),\tau(T_{0}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(Y),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(Y),\tau(T_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(Y),\tau(T_{0}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(S_{1},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(S_{0},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(X,Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(S_{1},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(S_{0},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(S_{1}),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(S_{0}),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(X),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(S_{1}),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(S_{0}),\tau(Y))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

All vertical arrows, except the middle arrows, are equivalences by assumption 3) and the fact that
T
is rigid. By the five lemma, the middle vertical arrows above are also equivalences.

Similar arguments apply to the following two diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(T_{0},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(T_{1},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(X,Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(T_{0},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(T_{1},Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(T_{0}),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(T_{1}),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(X),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(T_{0}),\tau(Y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(T_{1}),\tau(Y))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(Y,S_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(Y,S_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(Y,X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(Y,S_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(Y,S_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(Y),\tau(S_{0}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(Y),\tau(S_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(Y),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(T),\tau(S_{0}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(T),\tau(S_{1}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

We use the above constructed equivalences to show that
\tau
is fully faithful on Homs and exact extensions. Let
X,X^{\prime}\in C
. By
Lemma

5.16
, there exist exact sequences
T_{1}\to T_{0}\to X
and
T_{1}^{\prime}\to T_{0}\to X^{\prime}
in
C
with
T_{0},T_{1},T_{0}^{\prime},T_{1}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Add}(T)
. We form the following diagrams with horizontal exact sequences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(X^{\prime},T_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(X^{\prime},T_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(X^{\prime},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(X^{\prime},T_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(X^{\prime},T_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(X^{\prime}),\tau(T_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(X^{\prime}),\tau(T_{0}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(X^{\prime}),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(X^{\prime}),\tau(T_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(X^{\prime}),\tau(T_{0}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(T_{0}^{\prime},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(T_{1}^{\prime},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(X^{\prime},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(T_{0}^{\prime},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(T_{1}^{\prime},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(T_{0}^{\prime}),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}(\tau(T_{1}^{\prime}),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(X^{\prime}),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(T_{0}^{\prime}),\tau(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{E}(\tau(T_{1}^{\prime}),\tau(X))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The above shows that the non-central vertical morphisms are equivalences, and the five lemma yields that the central morphisms are also isomorphisms, showing the desired fully faithfulness of
\tau
.
∎

.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 17.7 -9.8 C 5.75 -16.7 -5.75 -16.7 -17.22 -10.08}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-0.86603}{0.5}{-0.5}{-0.86603}{-12.62093pt}{-7.18193pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

with
|a|=0,\,|a^{*}|=1,\,|l|=2
and differential determined on the generators via
d(l)=a^{*}a
and
d(a)=d(a^{*})=0
. We show in the following that the homology of
\mathscr{G}
is generated by
e_{1},e_{2},a,b
, with
e_{i}
the lazy path at
i
, and thus finite dimensional. It follows that
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G})=\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G})
and that the associated generalized cluster category
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(\mathscr{G})/\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G})
vanishes.

Let
X\in\mathscr{G}
be a cycle, meaning that it satisfies
d(X)=0
. We may split
X=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}X_{i,j}
, with
X_{i,j}
a cycle consisting of paths beginning at the vertex
i
and ending at the vertex
j
. We can write
X
as a
k
-linear sum of the generators, which are the paths of
\tilde{Q}
. We may thus write
X_{1,1}
as
X_{1,1}=lY^{1}+a^{*}aY^{2}+\lambda e_{1}
, with
Y^{1},Y^{2}\in\mathscr{G}
and
\lambda\in k
. Evaluating the condition
d(X_{1,1})=0
, we find
a^{*}aY^{1}-a^{*}ad(Y^{2})=0
, hence
Y_{1}=d(Y^{2})
, and thus get that
X_{1,1}=d(lY^{2})+\lambda e_{1}
is homologous to
\lambda e_{i}
. Similarly, we may write
X_{1,2}=aY
for
Y
some cycle consisting of paths beginning and ending at
1
. By the previous argument
Y
is homologous to
e_{1}
, and
X_{1,2}
is hence homologous to
a
. Analogous arguments show that
X_{2,1}
and
X_{2,2}
are homologous to
a^{*}
or
e_{2}
.

8.2
The stable category of
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
and the mapping class group action

Let
{\bf S}
be a marked surface and
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
the associated (not
\operatorname{Ind}
-complete) generalized cluster category, considered as a Frobenius exact
\infty
-category. The associated stable category
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
is a stable
\infty
-category whose triangulated homotopy
1
-category
\operatorname{ho}\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
is equivalent to the stable category of the Frobenius extriangulated category
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
. By
Theorem

8.4
,
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{\operatorname{c}}
is equivalent as an extriangulated category to the Higgs category. As shown in
[
Wu23
, Cor. 5.19]
, the stable category
\operatorname{ho}\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
is thus equivalent to the usual triangulated
2
-Calabi–Yau cluster category associated with
{\bf S}
, considered for instance in
[
BZ11
]
.

We further note that the mapping class group action on
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}
induces an action on the stable
\infty
-category
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
.

Proposition 8.9
.

The mapping class group action on
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
from
Theorem

4.15
induces an action on
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
by automorphisms in
\operatorname{ho}\operatorname{St}
, and thus also an action on
\operatorname{ho}\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{\bf S}
by equivalences of triangulated categories.

Proof.

The action of the mapping class group does not affect the values of the global sections at the external edges of the auxiliary trivalent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
. It follows that the action preserves the set
W
from
Proposition

5.5
and hence induces an action on the localization.
∎

8.3
Generalized
n
-cluster categories of marked surfaces

The study of
2
-Calabi–Yau cluster categories admits a generalization to triangulated
n
-Calabi–Yau categories with
n\geq 2
, called
n
-cluster categories. Their representation theoretic behavior is very similar to that of the
2
-cluster categories. For example, there is a notion of
n
-cluster tilting objects which admit mutations similar to the
2
-Calabi–Yau case.
n
-Cluster categories for
n\geq 2
however do not categorify cluster algebras. They can be constructed in similar ways as the
2
-cluster categories, for example as orbit categories or explicit geometric constructions. Amiot’s construction of the generalized cluster category can also be used to construct
n
-Calabi–Yau categories with
n
-cluster tilting objects, see
[
Guo11
]
. A possible input for this is a higher Ginzburg algebra, meaning an
(n+1)
-Calabi–Yau version of the usual Ginzburg algebra.

We fix a marked surface
{\bf S}
and choose an
n
-valent spanning graph
\mathcal{T}
of
{\bf S}
. Its dual describes an
n
-angulation (decomposition into
n
-gons) of the surface. As shown in
[
Chr21
]
, there is an associated relative higher Ginzburg algebra
\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}}
and a
\mathcal{T}
-parametrized perverse schober
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}
with global sections its derived
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})
. It’s generalized cluster category is given by the Verdier quotient

\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})/\operatorname{Ind}\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})\,,

we refer to
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
as the generalized
n
-cluster category for clarity. In the following, we survey how the results of this article generalize to
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
.

Locally at each vertex of
\mathcal{T}
, the spherical adjunction underlying the perverse schober
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}
is given by

f^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}(k)\longleftrightarrow\operatorname{Fun}(S^{n},\mathcal{D}(k))\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}f_{*}\,,

where
f
denotes the map
S^{n}\rightarrow\ast
. We now proceed in analogy with
Section

4
. Under the equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\operatorname{Fun}(S^{n},\mathcal{D}(k))\simeq\mathcal{D}(k[t_{n-1}])
, the functor
f^{*}
is identified with the pullback functor
\phi^{*}
along
\phi\colon k[t_{n-1}]\xrightarrow{t_{n-1}\mapsto 0}k
. By
Lemma

2.11
, the quotient
\mathcal{D}(k[t_{n-1}])/\operatorname{Ind}\mathcal{D}(k[t_{n-1}])^{\operatorname{fin}}
is equivalent to the derived
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(k[t_{n-1}^{\pm}])
of
(n-1)
-periodic chain complexes.

Theorem 8.10
.

There exist a vanishing-monadic and nearby-monadic
\mathcal{T}
-parametrized perverse schober
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{mnd}}
and a locally constant
\mathcal{T}
-parametrized perverse schober
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{clst}}
with generic stalk
\mathcal{D}(k[t_{n-1}^{\pm}])
satisfying the following.

i)

There is a semiorthogononal decomposition
\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{clst}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{mnd}}\}
of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

ii)

There exists an equivalence

\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{mnd}})\simeq\operatorname{Ind}\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})\,.

iii)

There exists an equivalence

\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{clst}})\simeq\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})/\operatorname{Ind}\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(\mathscr{G}_{\mathcal{T}})\,.

Proof.

Analogous to the proofs of
Proposition

4.5
and
Theorem

4.8
.
∎

We thus find the generalized
n
-cluster category
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
to be equivalent the topological Fukaya category of
{\bf S}
with coefficients in the derived
\infty
-category of
(n-1)
-periodic chain complexes. In particular, the well-known
2
-periodic topological Fukaya category of a surface may thus be seen as the generalized
3
-cluster category of the surface.

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
is smooth and proper as an
(n-1)
-periodic, or
2(n-1)
-periodic if
n
is even, stable
\infty
-category. The functor

\prod_{e\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\partial}}\operatorname{ev}_{e}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}=\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{clst}})\longrightarrow\prod_{e\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\partial}}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\operatorname{clst}}(e)\simeq\prod_{e\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\partial}}\mathcal{D}(k[t_{n-1}^{\pm}])

further admits an
n
-Calabi–Yau structure, see
[
Chr23
, Thm. 6.1]
.

A geometric model for
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
, describing (a priori a subset of) objects in
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
in terms of matching data with local value
k[t_{n-1}^{\pm}][i]
,
0\leq i\leq n-2
, is given in
[
Chr21
]
. The main difference to the geometric model for
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
is thus that we may associate objects to matching curves equipped with a grading datum in
\mathbb{Z}/(n-1)\mathbb{Z}
. Note that
[
Chr21
]
only describes some of the morphism objects between the associated global sections (because the matching curves are not necessarily pure). One can prove analogs of
Theorems

6.7
and
6.8
using a variation of the approach in
[
Chr21
]
applied directly to
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
. Furthermore, the proof of the geometrization
Theorem

6.12
applies with minor modifications also to
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
, showing that all compact objects in
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n}
arise from collections of graded matching curves in
{\bf S}\backslash M
.

As in
Section

5.2
, we can use the functor
\prod_{e\in\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\partial}}\operatorname{ev}_{e}
to define an exact
\infty
-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{\bf S}^{n,\operatorname{c}}
and hence also an extriangulated structure on the homotopy category
\operatorname{ho}\mathcal{C}^{n,\operatorname{c}}_{\bf S}
. This exact
\infty
-structure is again Frobenius.
